# Repair Ledger — the running tally of fixes (audit against reverts)

**Why this exists (Eric, 2026-07-27):** "Earlier I saw Draw was fixed, now it's messed up
again. What would help is a ledger of repairs — a running tally we can audit to make sure
nothing reverted." Concurrent Claude sessions share one checkout and switch branches under
each other; a fix can silently disappear, or a **stale preview** can *look* like a revert when
the code is actually fine. This ledger is the source of truth for "is fix X still in?"

## How to use it

- **Every non-trivial fix/feature gets ONE row here, in the same commit as the fix.** No row =
  not shipped, same rule as slash commands in CLAUDE.md.
- Each row carries a **PROOF anchor**: an exact string that must be present (`grep`-able) in a
  named file. That's what makes the ledger *auditable* rather than a memory.
- **To audit** (catch reverts): run `npm run ledger:audit` (or `node scripts/audit-repair-ledger.mjs`).
  It re-greps every proof anchor against `main` (or the current tree) and reports any that
  VANISHED — those are real reverts. A green audit means every recorded fix is still present.
- **A stale/old PREVIEW is not a revert.** Before filing "X reverted", confirm the preview URL
  is built from a commit that *contains* the fix — the audit checks the CODE, not a screenshot.
- Newest at the top. Never delete a row; if a fix is intentionally rolled back, add a new row
  documenting the rollback and set the old row's Status to `SUPERSEDED by <date>`.

## Columns

`Date` · `Area` · `Fix` · `Proof anchor` (string → file) · `Verified` (how proven) · `Status`

---

| 2026-08-06 | Product Intelligence ignored the day-range toggle — its win rankings were all-time while the rest of the dashboard was window-scoped (Eric: "yes" to window-scoping PI). In the Mission Control ship I flagged this as a gap needing a won_at timestamp. It turned out NOT to be a gap: pipeline_history already records every stage change via the record_pipeline_stage_change DB trigger, so a won pursuit has a real (to_stage=won, changed_at) row — the win date, no migration. Wired REAL window-scoping: read the won pursuits pipeline ids -> pipeline_history WHERE to_stage=won, map pipeline_id -> latest changed_at (a re-open->re-win keeps the most recent), then keep only wins whose won-transition is >= sinceIso (the same window the day-range toggle sets). { data, error } bound + surfaced on every read (user_pipeline, pipeline_history, sam_opportunities) — never ?? 0. A win with NO history row (pre-trigger data) CANNOT be dated: it is EXCLUDED from the window AND DISCLOSED as wonUndated (a count + a UI line "+N older wins predate the stage-history log, excluded not hidden") — never silently kept (which would leak all-time wins into a 7-day view) nor silently dropped (which would hide that the window count is incomplete). Response gained windowScoped:true + wonUndated; wonCount is now the IN-WINDOW count; grounded:false when zero wins fall in the window. Client card sub-line reads "N won pursuits in the last <days> days" + the undated disclosure. READ-side only; no migration, no emitter change. | `pipeline_history` -> `src/app/api/admin/map-funnel/route.ts` · `windowScoped` -> `src/app/admin/map-funnel/page.tsx` | tsc 0; funnel-logic units 33/33 (31 + 2: window-scopes by won-transition so a 100-day-old win does NOT leak into a 30d view -> wins:1 not 2; an undated/pre-trigger win is EXCLUDED from the window + counted as wonUndated:1, never ranked). Grounding verified by hand: pipeline_history is a real table with a live trigger (migration 20260410_pipeline_tracker.sql: to_stage/changed_at + record_pipeline_stage_change); all three reads bind {data,error} + surface; latest-changed_at-wins de-dupes re-wins; undated disclosed not hidden. | 2026-08-05 | Pursuits kebab: ALL row menus render always-open (Eric screenshot #2 — 3 menus stacked, AFTER my "sweep" fix deployed) — the ACTUAL root cause | THE REAL BUG (my two prior menu fixes MISSED it): `.km-pop` sets `display:flex`, which OVERRIDES the `[hidden]` attribute's UA `display:none` (a class rule beats the attribute's built-in style). So every menu was ALWAYS visible — `hidden` was set correctly on the element but did nothing, stacking every row's menu at once. The JS toggle/sweep was irrelevant because the menus were never actually hidden by CSS. FIX: one line — `.km-pop[hidden]{display:none}` so the attribute actually hides. WHY MY EARLIER TESTS PASSED WHILE THE BUG SHIPPED (the real lesson): they asserted on the `[hidden]` ATTRIBUTE (`querySelectorAll('.km-pop:not([hidden])')`) — which was always correct — NOT on the COMPUTED display. A menu can carry `hidden` yet still render if CSS forces display. The correct test checks `getComputedStyle(el).display !== 'none'`. SUPERSEDES the "sweep every .km-pop" fix (that hardening is harmless + kept, but it was NOT the cause). | `.km-pop[hidden]{display:none}` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/pursuits/route.ts` | tsc 0; headless with the CORRECT test (computed display, not the attribute): 0 menus VISUALLY visible on load (was 3 = the screenshot), open A1 -> 1 visible, open A2 -> still 1; 0 emoji, 0 mojibake. | 2026-08-04 | M-Win / Pursue showed "Sign in to see your fit" to a SIGNED-IN user (Eric: "i am already signed in") | Root cause = the drawer's _uemail() decodes the WRONG JWT segment (t.split('.')[0] = the HEADER, but the email lives in [1], the PAYLOAD — verifyTwoFactorSessionToken reads encodedPayload=[1]). So _uemail() returned '' even with a valid token, and TWO gates then treated the authed user as signed out: (a) loadMWin short-circuited on if(!em) before even fetching, and (b) the route checked if(!email) FIRST and returned reason:'signed_out'. | 2026-08-03 | M-Estimate restamp timed out on the whole-table UPDATE | The single predicate UPDATE (#829) fixed the URL-length 500 but hit the NEXT failure: a ~10k-row UPDATE over the Supabase API connection exceeds statement_timeout ("canceling statement due to statement timeout"). Two failure modes to thread between — a 1000-id .in() blows the URL cap, a whole-table UPDATE blows the statement timeout. Fix: page in SMALL chunks of 200 ids (short URL AND fast per-UPDATE), loop within a 240s soft budget under the 300s function cap, return remaining so a re-run finishes any tail. Idempotent, admin-gated, refreshes a derived cache only. | `const CHUNK = 200` → `src/app/api/cron/precompute-opp-intel/route.ts` | tsc 0; supabase-errors gate OK; 200-id chunks under both URL + statement-timeout limits | PENDING deploy + trigger |
| 2026-08-03 | M-Estimate restamp 500'd on the 1000-id .in() list | The ?restamp branch (PR #828) paged the stamped rows then cleared them via .update().in('notice_id', chunk) with a 1000-element array. PostgREST renders .in() into a notice_id=in.(id1..id1000) query STRING that exceeds the URL length cap, so it 400s upstream and surfaced as HTTP 500 "Bad Request" (the read-side .select().limit(1000) succeeded, which is why the auth/select looked fine). Rewrote to a single PREDICATE-based UPDATE: count via head:true, then update by the same WHERE (active + response_deadline>now + intel_computed_at IS NOT NULL) — Postgres runs it server-side over the indexed predicate, no id list, no URL-length limit. cntErr is bound + surfaced so the count-null gate stays clean. | `.update({ intel_computed_at: null })` → `src/app/api/cron/precompute-opp-intel/route.ts` | tsc 0; supabase-errors gate OK (cntErr bound); no id enumeration | PENDING deploy + trigger |

| 2026-08-03 | M-Estimate: every same-NAICS card showed the IDENTICAL number — sub-agency narrowing was self-nulling on an agency string mismatch | Eric: "there are a lot of cards with the same numbers." Root cause (MEASURED, not guessed): getComparableAwardRange's progressive narrowing tried the sub-agency tier as run(agency, sub) — passing BOTH the agency AND the sub-agency filter. But SAM's sub_tier/department strings and USASpending's awarding_agency strings DISAGREE on format: SAM sends "INTERIOR, DEPARTMENT OF THE", USASpending stores "Department of the Interior", and opp_value_range matches with ILIKE (case-insensitive but not word-order). So awarding_agency ILIKE 'INTERIOR, DEPARTMENT OF THE' matched 0 rows → the (agency IS NULL OR agency ILIKE p_agency) predicate was false for every comparable → the sub tier returned n=0 even though a GOOD sub-agency band existed (BLM 237990 = $1.76M across 15 real comps; Forest Service $784K; NPS $5.7M) → fell all the way through to the NAICS-wide median ($4.9M) on EVERY Interior/DoD card. Verified in the live DB via supabase-readonly: stamped intel_value_range.median = 4902177 (NAICS-wide) on BLM/Reclamation/NPS/Forest-Service 237990 opps that SHOULD differ. FIX: the sub-agency tier now matches on SUB-AGENCY ALONE — run(null, sub, …) — because the sub-agency name uniquely identifies the buying command without needing the parent department pinned on top (and it ILIKE-matches cleanly). Agency + NAICS-only fallbacks unchanged. NOTE: this is a CODE fix; the stored intel_value_range across active opps is STALE and needs a re-stamp (clear intel_computed_at → re-run precompute-opp-intel) to take effect on the cards — a bulk write, done post-deploy with Eric's OK. Also confirmed PSC-keying was the WRONG fix (psc_code only 8.6% populated in recompete_opportunities → 91% would fall back to NAICS median anyway; measured before building). | `if (sub) { const r = await run(null, sub,` -> `src/lib/opportunities/value-range.ts` | tsc 0; 1696/1696 tests incl. value-range.unit.test.ts new lock (sub tier = run(null,sub); NOT run(agency\|\|null,sub)); root cause measured live (BLM sub-band $1.76M/15 comps vs stamped NAICS-wide $4.9M) | PENDING deploy + re-precompute |

| 2026-08-02 | Chrome STILL offered saved passwords on the search bar — nuclear anti-autofill guard | Screenshot: Chrome's password-manager dropdown appeared on the opportunity-search input offering saved emails (eric@govcongiants.com, demo@getmindy.ai) + Manage Passwords — despite the earlier type=search + autocomplete=off + data-1p-ignore fix. Chrome treats a lone text input as a username field and offers CREDENTIALS. The reliable defeat is THREE layers: (1) two OFF-SCREEN decoy inputs — name=username autocomplete=username + name=password autocomplete=current-password, positioned left:-9999px, aria-hidden, tabindex=-1 — that Chrome fills INSTEAD, leaving the real box alone; (2) the real search input starts readonly (Chrome will not autofill a readonly field) and an inline onfocus strips readonly on first focus so typing works; (3) autocomplete=off + data-1p-ignore + data-form-type=other. The inline onfocus coexists with the existing addEventListener('focus') search-panel handler (both fire). | data-form-type="other" -> src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts | drawer-JS gate 9/9 clean; tsc 0; decoy pair + readonly-until-focus + ignore attrs; search-panel focus handler intact | PENDING deploy |

| 2026-08-02 | Chrome autofilled the user's EMAIL into the map's opportunity-search + filter inputs | Eric screenshot: eric@govcongiants.com appeared as the VALUE of the top opportunity-search bar. Cause: the input already had autocomplete=off, but Chrome IGNORES autocomplete=off on a bare text input (no type, no name) and heuristically fills anything that looks like a contact field near the top of the page. Fix: the search input gets type=search + name=opps-q + autocorrect/autocapitalize/spellcheck off + data-1p-ignore + data-lpignore (type=search signals a SITE search, not a form field — this is what actually stops Chrome; autocomplete=off alone does not). Hardened the same way for the 6 Filters-panel inputs (NAICS/PSC/FSC/Agency/Sub-agency/State) which had the identical vulnerability — the 2-char State field was especially exposed. The login-modal email input KEEPS autocomplete=email (that one SHOULD autofill). | 2026-08-02 | Awards ingest --apply WIRED + proven end-to-end (BQ awards freshness) | Wired the real download→load→MERGE in scripts/ingest-usaspending-awards.ts (was a dry-run scaffold). Flow: POST USASpending async bulk-download → poll status_url until 'finished' → download+unzip the contract CSV → `bq load --autodetect --replace` into awards_ingest_staging → MERGE on txn_id into the awards table. PROVEN on a tiny 2-day window (2026-07-28→31, 27,475 real transactions): MERGE affected 27,475 rows, watermark advanced 2026-04-23→2026-07-31, and 238910 now returns real awards (Todd Bonney Trucking $54K Marion MT / Interior) with recipient+$+city+state — the Past Awards payload. Three real bugs found+fixed while proving it: (1) bq load target must be dataset.table not project.dataset.table (doubled the project → 'Dataset not found'); (2) BQ autodetect read leading-zero code columns (naics_code '097', awarding_agency_code) as INT64 → CAST every code column to STRING in the MERGE SELECT (naics is the primary filter key — a silent type flip there is the worst case); (3) the MERGE scanned the whole 63M-row target (~43GB, blew the app bqQuery 5GB READ cap) → run the MERGE via the `bq query` CLI (uncapped, correct tool for bulk DDL) AND bound the target scan with T.action_date >= start (partition-prune). DRY-RUN still default (no --apply = zero writes). (5) PRICING — get_pricing_intel keeps grounded/degraded DISTINCT: a real NAICS (541512) is grounded=true; a no-input call is an honest empty (grounded=false, degraded=false, validation_error=no_input); an ERRORED CALC is degraded=true. The documented trap this guards: GSA CALC is keyless + IP-rate-limited, and a 429 swallowed to null would report grounded=false/degraded=false — INDISTINGUISHABLE from a genuine "no rates," so an agent tells the user no pricing exists when CALC merely throttled. (Confirmed the tool is sound: degraded = fetchErrored && !grounded, and a WARM 12h cache legitimately serves real data even when CALC is down, which is why a cold-error is the only degraded path.) (6) M-WIN — a PARTIAL match (same NAICS+agency as the strong match, but wrong set-aside + no capability + huge contract) must keep its earned NAICS+agency credit (≥40) and land strictly between the no-profile floor (30) and the strong match (98). This is the GRADIENT guard that verify:m-scale's strong-total(98)+fallback+coarse-monotonic checks DON'T cover — real users' opps are almost never perfect matches, so a weight drift that leaves the strong 98 intact but collapses the middle band would pass m-scale yet break everyone. PROVEN at the SOURCE: drift the real scoreAgencyExperience exact-match weight 15→0 → partial drops 52→37 (< 40 floor) → FAIL; revert → pass. | 2026-08-02 | Oracle-test coverage for the 4 high-stakes NON-search surfaces (bid-decision correctness) | Eric: "what other places should we run oracle tests?" The search 1000-row-cap bug passed 11 unit tests + a green build + a LOCAL run against live data — only a LIVE prod check caught it. So the value of an oracle is: hit the live surface + assert CORRECTNESS vs an independent ground truth, not "returns 200/non-empty." Built `npm run verify:oracles` (scripts/verify-oracles.mjs) covering the four surfaces where a confident-but-wrong output changes a BID decision AND a green build would miss it: (1) SCOPE — a state-scoped contractor search returns firms genuinely IN that state, not the national top-N-by-$ (the map "national whales" starvation that hit 3×); (2) REPORT — generate_market_report's agency table uses the canonical 3-FY MARKET_SPEND_WINDOW, not a 1-FY window (the documented, unfixed mismatch — the oracle quantifies it: 1-FY=$28.6B vs correct 3-FY=$86.0B for 541512); (3) CONTACTS — a known DoDAAC (W912PL → LA District) returns THAT office's real @usace.army.mil roster (10/10), not a dept-wide osd.osbp fallback; (4) ALERT — normalizeNAICSForPersist keeps a 6-digit code EXACT (541512→1 code), never fanning to the 51-code 541 family (the cross-industry alert flood). Each check calls the PURE fn against LIVE data (like verify:search) so auth-gating doesn't matter, and each is PROVEN by inject→red→revert→green (scope: drop scope → 15/15 off-state leaks; alert: query-time expander → 51 codes; contacts: dept-wide → 0 usace + fallback=true; report: 1-FY → canonical=false). | 2026-08-02 | Search ranking saw only 1000 of ~2500 matches (arbitrary cap) — page the FULL matching set | LIVE-VERIFY of the IDF ranking (below) exposed the REAL root cause: prod STILL showed "SERVER FRONT"/"Septic Tank" on top even though the deploy ran the IDF code. Because ranking can only order what it's GIVEN — and the mi-dashboard route fetched the filtered light rows with `.range(0, 5999)` but NO `.order()`, and PostgREST HARD-CAPS a single response at 1000 rows. So Andre's query "cyber cloud compliance network server" (which matches 2,517 active notices, measured) had ranking applied to an ARBITRARY 1000-row slice — the genuine 4-5-term cyber/cloud opps were usually NOT in that slice, so they could never rank to page 1. This is the rank-then-filter/arbitrary-cap class: the fix that "surfaces the best matches" is inert if the best matches were never fetched. FIX: page through the filtered set 1000 at a time up to SCAN_CAP (6000) with a DETERMINISTIC order (posted_date desc, id desc tiebreak) so pagination doesn't skip/repeat and the freshest matches lead when the set exceeds the cap. Verified the builder is safe to re-`.range()` (3000 unique rows, no dupes/double-order). RESULT (full 2,517-row set, live corpus): top-10 avg terms-matched jumped 1.2 → 2.1 and real multi-term cyber opps ("Communications, Network, Engineering, Cybersecurity", "Cyber Future Capability Directorate RFI") surface. | `for (let off = 0; off < SCAN_CAP; off += PAGE)` -> `src/app/api/mi-dashboard/route.ts` | tsc 0; next build exit 0; 18 unit tests pass; measured true matching set = 2,517 (route saw 1,000); ranking over the FULL set lifts top-10 avg 1.2→2.1 terms and surfaces multi-term cyber opps | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-02 | Multi-word search RANKING surfaced boilerplate — IDF-weight the coverage (rare terms win) | Follow-on to the multi-word fix below: with the OR-fix live, Andre's query "cyber cloud compliance network server" returned 200 rows (good) but the TOP results were "70--SERVER FRONT, IN REPAIR", "48--VALVE,GLOBE", "Septic Tank" — a valve/hose solicitation that mentions "cyber" + "compliance" ONCE in a generic boilerplate clause ("contractor shall comply with cybersecurity requirements"). ROOT CAUSE: rankSearchResults counted every matched term EQUALLY (raw coverage), so a valve matching 2 boilerplate terms tied/beat a real cloud opp matching 2 distinctive terms. GROUND TRUTH (the authority when unsure how ranking should behave): BM25 / Elasticsearch / Postgres ts_rank ALL weight by IDF — a rare term is strong evidence, a common one is weak. Measured LIVE in the active corpus (35,643 notices): IDF=ln(N/df) → compliance 2.00 (df 4,833), network 2.84, cyber 3.32, server 4.20, cloud 4.44 — so compliance/network are the boilerplate half and must count for LESS. FIX: added COMMON_TERM_WEIGHT (a static, corpus-grounded stand-in for IDF down-weighting ~20 GovCon boilerplate words 0.3–0.5×; everything distinctive stays 1.0) and rankSearchResults now scores Σ(position × rarity) — a rare term in the TITLE dominates; a common term in the BODY barely registers. RESULT (live corpus, same query): top-8 went from valves/septic-tanks to "Industry Day Joint Cyber Facility", "Google Cloud Platform Enterprise Agreement", a 5/5-term "Tactical ATC Command and Control" — real cyber/cloud opps. Shared lib → map + app + saved-search-cron all inherit it. Single-word search still a no-op. | `COMMON_TERM_WEIGHT` -> `src/lib/mi-dashboard/search.ts` | tsc 0; 11 unit tests incl. a new "rare-term beats boilerplate at equal coverage" pin; 8/8 live search oracles pass (Andre top-10 avg 3.1 terms ≥ bottom-10 1.0); verified top-8 on the LIVE corpus are real cyber/cloud opps, not valves | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-02 | Multi-word search returned 0 results (Andre's real query) — now OR-terms + relevance rank | Eric: "test Andre searches against the search" + "build an oracle test." Built scripts/verify-search.mjs (npm run verify:search) — an ORACLE test that hits the LIVE search + asserts CORRECTNESS vs ground truth (not just non-empty). It IMMEDIATELY caught a real bug: Andre @ CypherIntel's actual query "cyber cloud compliance network server" returned ZERO rows because buildSearchOr matched the WHOLE phrase as one literal substring (title.ilike.%cyber cloud compliance network server%) — no notice has that string. GROUND TRUTH for the fix = how real search engines handle multi-word queries (Google / Postgres FTS / Elasticsearch ALL: tokenize → OR each term → RANK by relevance; literal-phrase is the one thing none of them do). Fix in the SHARED lib (so map + app + saved-search-cron all get it): queryWords() tokenizes (split on space/comma, drop stop-words + <2char, dedupe, cap 8); buildSearchOr ORs EACH word's clauses (+ its term-of-art aliases) instead of the literal phrase; rankSearchResults() scores by terms-matched × location (title 5 / SOW 3 / body 1) so multi-term title matches float to page 1 and single-weak-term body noise (a valve notice mentioning "server") sinks — applied in mi-dashboard BEFORE pagination. Single-word search is byte-identical (no-op rank). VERIFIED: Andre's query 0 → 200 rows, top-10 avg 3.6/5 terms ≥ bottom-10 1.0; 8/8 live oracles + 20 unit tests pass. | `rankSearchResults` -> `src/lib/mi-dashboard/search.ts` | tsc 0; next build exit 0; 20 unit tests (queryWords/buildSearchOr/rankSearchResults); 8/8 live search oracles incl. the Andre regression guard (0→200 rows, ranked) | PENDING deploy |

The department average (21.2%) describes almost nobody: of 470 scored offices, 46 are >=50% early while 115 post ZERO early notices (NAVAL AIR SYSTEMS COMMAND 89% vs DLA AVIATION 10%). Behavioural read is the product: high = the requirement can still be shaped, go build the relationship; low/none = nothing to influence, just bid it when it posts. THREE rules the data forced, not guessed: (1) denominator excludes Award Notice/Justification (not opportunities — including them understated every office, mean 19.0%->21.2%); (2) BANDS not a raw percent, because splitting at 2026-06-01 the mean per-office swing between periods is 10.7 points (438/600 within 15) so '89%' on a card implies precision the data lacks — the percent lives in the tooltip only; (3) sample floor of 8, since a 1-of-1 office scores '100% early' and is noise. Floor is enforced in BOTH the scorer and the view's early_signal_shown flag, with a test asserting they agree. Pins decorated at the MERGE point so SAM+DLA+SBIR all get it from one cached directory load (no per-pin query); ?early=high,medium filters, and unscored offices are EXCLUDED (unknown is not known-good). With ?early= active the headline counts fall back to the real returned count, else it would read '1,247 of 12,000' above 21 pins. Extracted the ingest core from scripts/ingest-grants.ts into a SHARED lib ingestGrants (src/lib/grants/ingest.ts) so the local runner AND the new cron call ONE code path (the extract-per-record-logic rule; same shape as ingestDibbs) — they can't drift. New route /api/cron/sync-grants: auth = ADMIN_PASSWORD or CRON_SECRET (free API, no money-guard urgency but a random caller shouldn't hammer Grants.gov through us); wraps ingestGrants (posted + forecasted); a 0-fetch run returns HTTP 500 + reportCronOutcome('error') so a silent upstream break shows in cron_jobs (Grants.gov always has 1,200+ posted), a partial `degraded` fetch stays a 200 success (next run finishes via upsert/dedupe). Ordered rollout per rule #5: route deploys FIRST, curl 200 on prod, THEN the cron_jobs row (daily) — never the reverse. | 2026-07-30 | MINDY-007 · THE REAL BLOCKER — raw-PIID contract_ids 404 (not a throttle) | The rounds of "throttle" pain (34%, then 100% failure batches) were MOSTLY MISDIAGNOSED. Root cause found by checking the actual HTTP status: contract_id is NOT always the generated_internal_id. Some rows store the full "CONT_AWD_<piid>_…" form (those 200 directly — the 680 that worked); MANY store a RAW PIID (FA824018C7218, DESC0014664), and /api/v2/awards/<rawPIID>/ returns 404 fast (a non-2xx, not a dropped connection — which is why it looked like a throttle). Fix: resolveRecompeteDetail now detects a non-generated id (no CONT_AWD_/CONT_IDV_/ASST_ prefix) and resolves it via the shared resolvePiidToId (search-based) BEFORE the detail fetch. A PIID that resolves to nothing is a GENUINE miss → return empty (STAMP it, don't retry a 404 forever); only a real fetch/network error retries. Also added a descriptive User-Agent to fetchAwardDetail (hygiene; USASpending was NOT actually UA-gating — verified — but it's correct + harmless). Verified live: 3 previously-404 raw PIIDs (FA824018C7218→psc R425, AIDOAATO1500007→R425, DESC0014664→U099) now resolve + return real detail; a 15-row --go run went 15/15 stamped, 0 failed (was 0/10 before). 745/132,711 done. | `resolveRecompeteDetail` -> `src/lib/recompete/detail-enrich.ts` | live: 3 raw-PIID 404s now resolve; 15/15 stamped 0 failed; 745 total | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-07-30 | MINDY-007 · LONG-TERM detail enrichment cron (self-healing) | The correct-in-the-long-term fix (Eric): psc/description shouldn't need a babysat one-off drain against a throttling endpoint. Cache investigation first (Eric: "we have this in cache already?") — NO free shortcut: mcp_external_cache has EDGAR/CALC/task-orders but NO award-detail; usaspending_awards is 847 unrelated rows (0 join hits); sam_opportunities HAS psc_code on 141K notices (97%) but PIID≠solicitation_number so it joins ~1/20 ([[piid_solnum_no_link]]). The detail endpoint (keyed on our contract_id) is the only source. So: a steady-state CRON /api/cron/enrich-recompete-detail drains detail_checked_at IS NULL rows in a SMALL serial batch per tick (never trips the throttle — the burst does), self-heals (a failed row stays NULL → retried next tick), AND covers rows the hourly sync ADDS going forward — permanently. Shared per-row logic extracted to src/lib/recompete/detail-enrich.ts (resolveRecompeteDetail with retry/backoff + stripNul + DetailFetchError), imported by BOTH the cron AND the local drain so they never drift. mode=preview default-safe, value-DESC ordering, soft wall-clock budget, ?stats=1 coverage view. Verified: shared lib returns real fields (psc 6640, desc) live; tsc clean. LIVE ON PROD: route verified execute 5/5, 100/100, 300/300 all clean 0-failed (300 rows in 122s, under the 300s cap); cron_jobs row inserted + verified (enabled=true, limit=300, 40 * * * *) after the route went live (deploy-route-first honored). 3,399/132,792 stamped (the high-value head); cron drains ~300/hr + fills future sync rows forever. | `enrich-recompete-detail` -> `src/app/api/cron/enrich-recompete-detail/route.ts` | live: route 300/300 clean; cron_jobs row enabled=true verified; 3,399 stamped | LIVE (cron scheduled) |
| 2026-07-30 | MINDY-007 remainder · recompete detail backfill (psc/description) | The un-derivable half of 007: psc_code / psc_description / description are NULL on all 132,693 live rows because USASpending's spending_by_award SEARCH endpoint returns them NULL even when requested (verified live — same class as NULL set-aside). The per-award DETAIL endpoint (/api/v2/awards/<id>/) DOES have them. New resumable local runner scripts/backfill-recompete-detail.ts REUSES the shared fetchAwardDetail (our contract_id column IS the generated_internal_id it wants) — no rebuilt fetch. DRY-by-default, --go gated, concurrency 5 (~10 req/s measured ceiling, $0 free API, ~3.7h full drain). Fail-loud: a transient fetch error LEAVES the row NULL for retry, never falsely stamps detail_checked_at (the silent-degradation guard). NUL-stripped. Migration 20260730_recompete_detail_checked_at.sql (hand-run + verified: column live). Proven on a 20-row --go pass: 19 persisted with real data (SSN 792 psc 1905, KC-X psc 1510, TRICARE Q201), 1 transient-fail correctly left unstamped. THROTTLE FINDING (2026-07-30): a short burst tolerates concurrency 5 (~10 req/s) but ~1000 SUSTAINED requests THROTTLE OUR IP — the endpoint drops connections (RemoteDisconnected), and even a serial known-good id then fails ~4-in-5. The burst run hit 34% failures at conc 5. So the full 132K drain must be POLITE. Runner upgraded: (1) internal PAGE loop (PostgREST hard-caps a select at 1000 rows, so --limit never fetched more in one shot) so one invocation drains fully; (2) retry-with-exponential-backoff per id (a dropped connection is transient — the same id succeeds after a pause) — defaults concurrency 2 / --delay 300ms / --retries 4; (3) a ZERO-PROGRESS guard that STOPS instead of spin-looping when a whole page fails. Bounded polite test (100 rows, conc 2, delay 300): 0 failures (vs 34% at conc 5). Strategy: partial top-VALUE drain (rows ordered by potential_total_value DESC → the visible/high-value contracts fill first). 680/132,693 stamped so far. | `backfill-recompete-detail` -> `scripts/backfill-recompete-detail.ts` | live: 20-row wrote 19; polite 100-row test 0 failures vs 34% at conc 5; 680 stamped | PARTIAL DRAIN (top-value, polite) |

## Other-session fixes (concurrent — email / billing / MCP)

Backfilled 2026-07-27: these landed on main from PARALLEL sessions while the map work was in flight.
The ledger must record them too, or a revert of one goes unnoticed (the ledger only catches what it
records). Anchors verified present in main at backfill time.

| Date | Area | Fix | Proof anchor | Verified | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-07-30 | MCP fidelity batch3 · MINDY-005 capability_market_match pharma collision | "market research" led NAICS ranking with 424210 Drugs & Druggists' (pharma), total $5.18B. THREE stacked bugs (all proven live): (1) the phrase collapsed to the lone word "market" — the ≥3× candidate override let "market" ($15.9B: aftermarket/stock-market pollution in Software Publishers + Petroleum) hijack the specific phrase "market research" ($913M). Fix: a single component word may NOT override a TWO-content-word phrase (rawContentCount ≥ 2); "demolition services" is safe (services is a generic tail → 1 content word → "demolition" still overrides). (2) even with the right phrase, the vocabulary lead-promotion certified 424210 as the lead because the bare word "market" maps to pharma in naics_vocabulary. Fix: for a multi-content-word query a code surfaced by only ONE lone word is uncorroborated (needs a bigram/alias hit OR ≥2 distinct words agreeing) and can't lead; a single-word query ("hvac") still trusts its dominant vocab hit. (3) promotion/injection now skip uncorroborated lone-word codes. Verified LIVE: market research → 541910 Marketing Research (pharma gone); hvac→238220, welding→333992, janitorial→561720, landscaping→561730, security guard→561612, drones→336411, demolition→562910 all unchanged; capability_market_match leads 541613 Marketing Consulting. | `MINDY-005` -> `src/lib/market/keyword-coverage.ts` | live: market research leads 541910 (was 424210 pharma); 8 regression keywords unchanged | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-07-30 | MCP fidelity batch3 · MINDY-010 get_contractor_award_history parent rollup | "Leidos" resolved to the subsidiary "LEIDOS BIOMEDICAL RESEARCH INC" (confidence medium) instead of parent Leidos, because ilike '%leidos%' accepted both the parent and token-superset subsidiaries and match.name was taken from the NEWEST award (a Biomedical Research one). Fix: when any accepted row is an EXACT normalized name match ('high': "Leidos" ≈ "LEIDOS, INC."), return ONLY those rows so the parent wins; fall back to the token-superset set only when there is no exact match (the ANDURIL #279 guard still holds — 'low' rows are already dropped). Verified LIVE: Leidos → "LEIDOS, INC." high $9.2B/22; Lockheed/Raytheon/Northrop/Booz/Deloitte all resolve to the PARENT at high confidence; General Dynamics honestly stays medium (no bare-parent row in cache); Anduril still correct (no #279 regression). | `MINDY-010` -> `src/lib/contractor-sales-history.ts` | live: Leidos→parent high; 5 primes→parent; Anduril guard intact | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-07-30 | MCP fidelity batch2 · MINDY-003 get_incumbent_financials name→CIK | "Owens & Minor" returned grounded=false / edgar=null. The suspected ampersand bug was WRONG (verified live): the name simply is not in company_tickers.json — that file is only the ~10K most-common tickers, and it MISSES many real 10-K filers (Owens & Minor = CIK 75252, files annually, absent). Fix adds a fallback that resolves the name through EDGAR's full company-name search (covers all ~800K filers), parsing the single-company atom block for cik + conformed-name. It even resolves through a rename (CIK 75252 now reports as "Accendra Health"). Tries raw plus & or and variants (EDGAR matches the LITERAL string, so & is not interchangeable with the word and). Also normalizes & to AND in normalizeName so ticker-index names like AT&T match. Honest miss preserved (fake private LLC → grounded=false, no fabricated CIK). Verified LIVE: "Owens & Minor" and "Owens and Minor" both → CIK 75252 FY2025 rev $2.76B; MSFT regression clean (FY2026 $331.8B). | `resolveCompanyViaEdgarSearch` -> `src/lib/edgar/index.ts` | live: Owens & Minor grounded=true $2.76B, both spellings resolve, MSFT clean, private=false | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-07-30 | MCP fidelity batch2 · MINDY-009 search_sam_opportunities relevance | keyword "market research" returned ambulances / borescopes / demolition — the tsvector weights title and description EQUALLY, so the phrase in a notice's boilerplate ("the Government will conduct market research…" on a Sources Sought) matched as strongly as a real Market-Research-services notice, and ordering by soonest deadline floated that boilerplate to the top. Fix runs a TITLE-scoped pass first (the phrase is what the notice is ABOUT), then fills the remainder with the existing tsvector/body pass, de-duped by solicitation_number, title hits on top. Migration-free (weighted tsvector + ts_rank_cd would be strictly better but needs a column rewrite + RPC). ## Guardrails / gates (meta-repairs)

| Date | Area | Fix | Proof anchor | Verified | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-18 | `backfill-sam-descriptions` reads as a DEAD CRON but is a COMPLETED one — do not "fix" it again | Surfaced while tracing what refreshes /today: this job shows `last_status = error`, `last_run_at = 2026-08-01`, i.e. 17 days stale, and looks exactly like rot. It is not. It is `enabled = false`, disabled deliberately at 2026-08-01 19:55 — the same day it last ran. `last_status` FREEZES at whatever the final run returned, so a retired job keeps advertising its last error forever (the same sticky-last_status pattern the watchdog rules already call out). WHAT IT DID: most `sam_opportunities` rows arrive with `description` set to a SAM API URL pointer rather than prose; this cron resolved them 50/run in the background so page loads landed on inline text instead of the on-demand "Load full description" fetch. WHY RETIRING IT WAS CORRECT, measured 2026-08-18: **0 of 35,378 ACTIVE rows still hold a URL pointer** — the work that matters is fully drained. Of 170,768 total rows, 130,202 (76%) carry real text and 20,558 (12%) are still pointers, but every one of those is on an inactive/archived row nobody reads; continuing would burn the shared SAM quota (1,000/day/key) resolving dead records. SEPARATE, NOT A BACKFILL GAP: 20,008 rows (12%) have a NULL description — the pointer job cannot help a row that never carried a description reference, so if anything matches on `description` those rows are invisible to it. Not investigated here. ACTION: none. Re-enabling this cron would spend quota on archived rows for zero user-visible gain. | `Schedule: hourly (see vercel.json)` -> `src/app/api/cron/backfill-sam-descriptions/route.ts` | Measured on prod Supabase: active rows with a `http%` description = **0/35,378**; corpus 170,768 total / 130,202 real text / 20,558 pointer / 20,008 NULL. cron_jobs row read directly: `enabled=false`, `updated_at=2026-08-01T19:55:17Z` vs `last_run_at=2026-08-01T03:00:20Z` — disabled AFTER the failing run, i.e. retired on purpose. | ✅ CLOSED — NOT A DEFECT |
| 2026-08-03 | SEVEN health crons, all green through a six-day outage | Eric asked how we could have caught the customer escalation bugs without the customer. Four of five were catchable from data already in the database — nothing was asking the question. Every existing health cron measures LIVENESS (did the job run, is the cache fresh, is the provider up) and none measured THROUGHPUT (of the work that should have been produced, how much actually was). The gap hid all four: SAM sync said healthy while ingesting 1,000 of 23,000; daily alerts said success while processing 880 of 1,594 users; the send guard recorded alerts as skipped that were generated and never sent; and 594 users had briefings enabled with no customer_classifications row so the cron could not see them. Not one reported an error. New daily digest runs four checks and posts to Slack EVERY DAY, healthy or not, because a monitor that only speaks when something breaks is indistinguishable from one that has itself broken. ORACLE-TESTED against live production and the oracle earned its keep: it flagged a send-guard mismatch (expected 48, got 4) where the ROUTE was right and the expectation was wrong (48 was a 14-day total, the check correctly measures 24 hours). Tests pin QUERY SHAPE rather than arithmetic, since the failure mode that matters is a check silently reading the wrong column and reporting a cheerful 100 percent forever. Explicitly does NOT catch the fifth bug (a Set-Aside dropdown sending the label HUBZone where SAM stores the code HZC) — nothing was empty or erroring, so that class still needs a human clicking through. | `MIN_COMPLETENESS` → `src/app/api/cron/throughput-digest/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; vitest 6/6; live oracle run confirmed SAM 4 percent (1000/23192), alerts 1000/1594, orphaned 594. | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-03 | SAM watchdog reported SUCCESS through a six-day outage | From 2026-07-29 to 08-03 the nightly full sync fetched 1,000 of about 23,000 opportunities and this watchdog called it healthy every single day. It scored cache AGE (did we sync recently) and never COMPLETENESS (did we sync everything) — the cache looked fresh because 1,000 rows had just been written while 22,000 were silently missing. A status of partial is also not counted by consecutiveFailures, which only counts failed, so the outage registered nowhere. Same shape as the DIBBS empty-errors bug: measuring whether the job RAN instead of whether it produced normal VOLUME. Now scores total_fetched over total_available on the last full run (under 50 percent costs 40 points and flips healthy to warning; under 90 percent costs 15) and prints the ratio in the alert body so the number is visible rather than just a score. Also widened the recentRuns select from status alone to include sync_type and the totals — a status-only select is what made this uncheckable. REPLAYED against the real history: Jul 27-28 (completed, 100 percent) stay healthy with no false alarm; Jul 29 through Aug 3 (partial, 4 percent) all six flip to WARNING. It would have alerted on Jul 29 instead of going unnoticed until a paying customer complained of empty results. Alerts already route to Slack via sendOpsAlert, not email, despite the legacy to: field name. | `completenessRatio` → `src/app/api/cron/sam-sync-watchdog/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; scoring replayed against 8 nights of real sam_sync_runs data (6 caught, 2 clean). | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-03 | SAM full sync ingested 1,000 of 23,000 opportunities EVERY NIGHT | A paying user reported almost no matches in his daily alert and an empty Today's Intel. His profile was fine (12 NAICS, 40 keywords, 24 PSC codes) and 932 open opportunities matched his NAICS over 30 days. The real cause was upstream: this route exported NO maxDuration, so it inherited Vercel's default function timeout and was KILLED about 8 seconds in, after exactly ONE page. Measured from sam_sync_runs: total_available 23,192, total_fetched 1,000, status partial, last_successful_offset stuck at 1000, api_calls_made 2, duration 8s. The 09:00 resume run inherited the same ceiling and fetched 0. Daily posting counts therefore looked like a collapse in federal procurement (165 on a Monday vs about 900 on prior weekdays) when it was really a truncated fetch. NOT the loop condition (maxRecords 50000, batchSize 1000, good for 23 pages) and NOT cron_jobs.timeout_ms=290000, which governs the dispatcher's wait rather than the function's own execution limit. SECOND DEFECT fixed in the same commit: the checkpoint only wrote every 5 pages, so a run killed on page 3 recorded nothing and resume restarted from a stale offset, re-covering old ground. Now checkpoints every page. RELATED, applied as a cron_jobs change: backfill-sam-attachments cut from 6x/day to 2x/day, since at MAX_PER_RUN=150 it was burning about 900 of the shared 4,000/day SAM quota and starving these syncs (a live delta test returned 429 Message throttled out). | `maxDuration` → `src/app/api/cron/sync-sam-opportunities/route.ts` | tsc exit 0. NOT verifiable live at commit time: SAM quota exhausted until 2026-08-04 00:00 UTC. Verify the 01:00 full sync reports status completed with total_fetched near total_available, not partial at 1,000. | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-01 | Agency-intel sync — a source returning 0 records reported SUCCESS | The Aug data-freshness alert on agency pain points (123d stale) surfaced a bigger problem than the staleness: syncAllSources reported an EMPTY errors array while ONE OF ITS THREE SOURCES HAD BEEN COMPLETELY DEAD SINCE APRIL. Each catch block only fired on a THROWN error, but every fetcher catches its own per-agency failures and returns an empty array — so a dead source is indistinguishable from a healthy one that legitimately found nothing. it-dashboard contributed 0 records for FOUR MONTHS while each sync said "success, 793 fetched" and looked healthy; only counting PER-SOURCE revealed one of three never contributed. Fix is general, not an it-dashboard patch: a runSource wrapper records per-source counts and pushes an error when a source returns 0 (skipped under dryRun, where fetchers return [] by design), so GovInfo/USASpending going quiet will be loud too; syncAllSources now returns a bySource map. it-dashboard UNWIRED from full syncs — its API host myit-api.cio.gov no longer resolves in DNS (verified: dig returns nothing, while itdashboard.gov itself is still HTTP 200, so the data likely moved to a GSA/OMB feed). The fetcher file is KEPT, not deleted: its endpoint shape + 24 CFO Act agency codes are what a re-sourcing effort needs, and it stays reachable via ?source=it-dashboard for diagnostics. Admin route now advertises a deadSources list so a dead source is VISIBLE rather than silently absent. TRADEOFF, deliberate: a source that legitimately returns 0 (e.g. USASpending early in a fiscal year) will now report an error — false alarm beats silent rot. Data itself also refreshed in the same branch: re-ran the real ingest (793 fetched then 450 upserted, 450 rows updated_at=2026-08-01) rather than re-merging stale inputs, since the upstream table was ITSELF last written 2026-04-19 and a merge alone would have regenerated an identical file and turned a truthfully-firing alert green. HONEST SCOPE: the GAO half produced ZERO new pain points — all +47 additions are FY2026 spending priorities from USASpending. | `runSource` → `src/lib/agency-intelligence/index.ts` | tsc exit 0; vitest 2/2 (zero-source-guard.unit.test.ts) — a 0-record source surfaces in the errors array with correct per-source counts, a fully-healthy sync reports no errors; prod ingest verified 450 rows updated_at=2026-08-01; merged JSON 307 agencies preserved (none lost), priorities 2611 to 2658 | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-07-29 | FM-U07 grants sort — SILENT-EMPTY regression from the first fix | Re-verify caught it: the first FM-U07 grants fix set `sortBy: keyword ? 'relevancy\|desc' : 'openDate\|desc'`, but the grants.gov endpoint the lib calls (apply07.grants.gov/grantsws) ONLY accepts DATE sort fields — a `relevancy` (or title/agencyCode) value returns hitCount:0 with errorcode:0, a SILENT EMPTY not an error. | 2026-08-02 | Ask Mindy — remove the entire teaching/podcast RAG corpus from the backend | Eric: the Ask Mindy chat I shipped answered with a wall of markdown plus podcast citation chips, none of which matched the approved vision, and then he was explicit — not just the podcast, the 8-year corpus training, anything not related to the data core should go, no one wants to learn here. So the chat route no longer retrieves ANY knowledge base: the two retrieval calls that fetched teaching chunks and podcast episode cards were deleted along with their context-block builder, citation-chip builder and chunk-to-citation mapper, the RAG limit and max-chars constants, and the RagChunkResult import. | 2026-08-02 | Market report forecast values — bare numbers weren't comma-delimited | Eric: "some of the numbers are not comma delimited can we fix this in the reports." agency_forecasts.estimated_value_range is FREE TEXT in inconsistent formats — some already human ("$3B - $3.9B", "> $250M - < $1B", "$1,000,000,000 to $1,250,000,000") and some BARE DIGIT STRINGS ("3000000000", "600000000", "1500000000"). The report printed value_range verbatim (value_range || money(value_max)), so a bare-number row rendered "3000000000" unreadable. New prettyRange() normalizes: empty → the numeric value_max abbreviated ($3.0B); a pure bare number → abbreviated the same way; human text → comma-format any leftover bare 7+ digit run while keeping the rest. Verified against the real messy values: 3000000000→$3.0B, 600000000→$600.0M, and every human range preserved unchanged. | 2026-08-03 | Market report — FORECASTS always 0 + uneven KPI grid | Eric: "the market report shows no forecast? … on the actual report the ux is bad." TWO bugs. (1) DATA: the forecast section called agencyForecasts with naics=primaryNaics (ONE code — 541511 of the 4-code DOD-IT search, missing 541519's 2,668 rows) AND agency=the toptier name ("Department of Defense"). agency_forecasts.source_agency is stored as SHORT CODES (NAVY/USACE/DOD/GSA…), so the toptier name matched NOTHING → the whole section zeroed even though 3,266 current/future forecasts exist for the union. Fixed: pass the FULL NAICS union (queryForecasts splits a comma list) and DROP the name-based agency filter (NAICS is the right forecast scope; the forecast table can't honor a toptier name). (2) UX: the summary KPI grid stretched every card to the tallest, and the "Top product (PSC)" value was the FULL PSC label ("DA01 — IT AND TELECOM - BUSINESS APPLICATION/… (LABOR)") stuffed into a number slot → it wrapped ~10 lines and dragged the row. Fixed: statCard gained an optional clamped sub-line (PSC card now shows the CODE as the value + the name as a 2-line-clamped sub); .summary got align-items:start so cards size to their own content; the value line ellipsis-truncates; the dead "Buying NAICS —" card is dropped when null. | agencyForecasts union -> `src/mcp/tools/market-report.ts` | tsc clean (excl. pre-existing @sparticuz/chromium devdep); forecast-value tests 4/4; DB-verified 3,266 union forecasts vs the old 0 | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-03 | Map "0 results / No opportunities match" on a FAILED fetch | Eric hit an empty CONUS map 3× — always right after a deploy. Not a data bug: the API is healthy on every path (profile 135, all 4,770). The viewport fetch in the map's live JS treated a FAILED fetch identically to a genuine empty result — each horizon's per-source `.catch` resolved to `{pins:[],total:0}` and the outer `.catch` silently cleared `busy`, so an all-failed fetch (network blip / mid-deploy chunk mismatch) blanked a populated map to a fake "No opportunities match". Fix: tag a failed source `failed:true` (both the `!success` branch and the `.catch`); before merging, if EVERY enabled horizon failed, DON'T reassign OPPS — keep the last-good render and show an honest "Couldn't load — Retry" banner (`_showFetchError`, inserted above the feed so cards survive; `_clearFetchError` on next good fetch). A genuine empty (fetch OK, 0 rows) still falls through to the honest "No opportunities match". Outer `.catch` + contacts-branch `.catch` also surface the banner. | `failed:true` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; 5 source-assert tests + simulated all-failed/genuine-empty/partial decision (4/4 correct) | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-03 | Map "Open: 0" — client `toRow` called a SERVER-only fn | Eric: "where are all the opps?" The Horizons dropdown showed Open 0 while the Open endpoint genuinely returned totalForFilters 5,170 (proven headless: every Open fetch parsed success:true tf:5170, yet `__horizonTotals.open` ended 0, NO page error — swallowed). Root cause = a regression from the DNA card slice (#833): `toRow` runs in the BROWSER, and the slice added `sapBuyerTier(p.agency)` inside it to compute the SB-friendly badge. `sapBuyerTier` is a SERVER-lib import → undefined in the client → ReferenceError → `toRow` threw → the open horizon's `.map(toRow)` rejected → the part hit `.catch` → `{total:0,failed:true}` → the merge wrote Open's total as 0. Recompete/Forecast use different `toRow` branches (no such call) so ONLY Open zeroed. Fix: compute `sbf` SERVER-SIDE on the pin (`sapBuyerTier(pin.agency)==='most'?1:0`, next to the existing `fits`), client `toRow` now reads `p.sbf`; removed the now-unused client import; hardened the merge so a failed/superseded part can't overwrite a real horizon total with 0. Lesson: NEVER call a server-lib fn from code that ends up in a client `<script>` template literal. | `sbf: sapBuyerTier(pin.agency)` -> `src/app/api/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; DNA + fit-chips tests 9/9 (assert server-side grounding + client makes no call); headless-verify Open=5,170 post-deploy | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-05 | Forecast drawer reordered into the 8-section reading order (Prepare to Win + Contacts) | Eric (verbatim): "Keep the same eight-section reading order across opportunity types, but frame every section for planned work. Do not show empty solicitation, attachment, compliance, or proposal states. Replace 'Win This Contract' with 'Prepare to Win,' and replace Bid / No-Bid with Track / Research / Start Capture logic. Forecasts are an early-capture product, not incomplete solicitations." The forecast drawer was a flat overview + a single fcDetailBox dump — it read like an incomplete open opp. Rebuilt forecastRender into the SAME eight sections as the open drawer, each forecast-framed: 1 Overview (purple range hero + facts) → 2 Should I pursue? (fcPursueSec = Track / Research / Start capture cards, NOT Bid/No-Bid — a forecast is a capture decision, no bid yet) → 3 Opportunity intelligence (#fcOppBox: what they need + published detail) → 4 Market intelligence (#fcMktBox: agency estimate + who holds it now = the displacement target; OMITTED when neither is real, no empty market shell) → 5 Buyer intelligence + CONTACTS (#fcBuyerBox: the POC to engage now + the agency roster via loadForecastRoster→loadRoster) → 6 Related opportunities (#xsellOpen open bids) → 7 Prepare to win (fcPrepareSec, renamed from "Win This Contract"; early-capture checklist, NOT the proposal workspace/compliance) → 8 Actions (fcActions: Track-led sticky bar, no Generate-proposal / no View-on-SAM). buildTabs groups gained the fc* anchors (fcdesc/fcmkt/fcpoc/fcwin) so the SAME eight tabs resolve for a forecast. loadForecastDetail fills the three async section boxes by id and fails soft to honest copy — never a dead solicitation/attachment/compliance shell. | `function forecastRender` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; drawer-js parse clean (10 templates); vitest 338/338 incl. new forecast-drawer-sections (8-section order + Track/Research/Start-capture not Bid-No-Bid + Prepare-to-win not proposal + Contacts wiring + no-empty-shell); headless render of fcPursueSec/fcPrepareSec/fcActions produced valid HTML with correct anchors | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-05 | "This page couldn't load" across a deploy — auto-recover on chunk-load error | Eric hit Next's raw "This page couldn't load" on /app (screenshot at 06:56, EXACTLY mid the forecast-drawer deploy 11s before a healthy shot at 06:57). Not a code bug: across a Vercel build swap, a tab holding the OLD page requests JS chunks by their old content-hash; the new build no longer has those hashes → ChunkLoadError → the raw fallback. One reload fetches the new chunks. Added auto-recovery: (1) `src/app/global-error.tsx` — the ROOT boundary (catches errors even in the root layout, which a segment error.tsx can't); on a CHUNK error ONLY it auto-reloads ONCE (sessionStorage once-guard `mindy_chunk_reloaded`), else shows a branded fallback (Reload / Go to Mindy). (2) `src/components/ChunkReloadGuard.tsx` — belt-and-suspenders window 'error'/'unhandledrejection' listener for chunk failures that fire OUTSIDE React render (lazy import in a handler, prefetch, CSS chunk) and never reach the boundary; mounted in the root layout, SAME once-guard so the two can't double-reload. Invariants (unit-tested): reload ONLY on a chunk error (a TypeError/ReferenceError must reach the normal boundary, never reload); AT MOST ONCE (a genuinely broken build that re-throws post-reload does NOT loop); FAIL SAFE when storage is unavailable (privacy mode → no auto-reload). NOTE: /opportunity-map is a route.ts (raw HTML, no React), so this boundary doesn't apply there — its transient-failure handling is the existing in-page "Couldn't load — Retry" banner. | `mindy_chunk_reloaded` -> `src/components/ChunkReloadGuard.tsx` | tsc exit 0; FULL next build succeeded (global-error + layout compile); vitest 2014/2014 incl. new chunk-reload-guard (detector only-chunk + once-guard + fail-safe + wiring) | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-05 | /app crashed to the error boundary for every signed-in user — React #310 (hook after early return) | Eric hit "This page couldn't load" on /app repeatedly. NOT a chunk-swap (that was the wrong first diagnosis — a logged-OUT headless load was clean; only the SIGNED-IN render broke, deterministically). DevTools showed Minified React error #310 ("rendered more hooks than during the previous render") with a useState to useEffect stack. Root cause: PR #948 added an impressionSent useState plus a useEffect AFTER TargetingCard's return-null guard (if loading or no data). First render (loading=true) returned before those hooks; the next render (targeting data loaded) ran them, so the hook COUNT changed, triggering #310 — the whole dashboard tree threw to global-error. It only fired for signed-in users because it needs data to load past the guard (logged-out never reaches it). Fix: moved the impression useState + useEffect ABOVE the return-null guard so every hook runs unconditionally on every render (the effect keeps its own loading/no-data early-return so the SEND still fires once-per-load). WHY THE TOOLCHAIN MISSED IT: a hooks-order violation is a RUNTIME error — tsc, a green next build, and the source-assert unit tests all passed. Added a static guard the pipeline lacked: a new targeting-card-hooks unit test asserts NO hook call sits after the return-null guard (strips comments first so the file's own #310 explanation prose can't fool the scan). ESLint react-hooks rules-of-hooks clean on the file. NOTE: the global-error boundary + ChunkReloadGuard shipped just before were NOT wasted — they turned Chrome's raw crash page into the branded "Something went wrong" fallback that MADE this diagnosable, and still auto-recover real chunk swaps. | `const [impressionSent, setImpressionSent] = useState(false)` -> `src/components/app/panels/TargetingCard.tsx` | tsc exit 0; ESLint rules-of-hooks clean; FULL next build 546/546 pages; vitest 2017/2017 incl. new targeting-card-hooks guard | PENDING deploy |

### 2026-08-07 — ONE Institute: the 6 marketing papers become a labeled Research Backlog

Two conflicting "Institutes" existed: the Observatory-grounded `/research` (publications citing OBS-### standards) and the older `/institute` gallery of 6 ungrounded marketing "White Papers." Per Eric — "there can only be one Institute; the Observatory is the only source of authority" AND "never hide truthful content, always label it accurately" — the 6 papers are NOT deleted or noindexed. They're relabeled as **Research Concepts** awaiting a named Observatory standard (or "standard not yet defined" where none exists — never an invented OBS id), each carrying a visible status banner + Publication Status footer linking to `/research/how-we-publish`.

- `Research Concept` -> `src/lib/gov/paper.ts` (`conceptBanner`/`conceptFooter`/`CONCEPT_CSS`, injected by `renderPaper` when `meta.concept` is set)
- `meta.concept` -> `src/lib/gov/papers-registry.ts` (4 papers: bidder-pool→OBS-009, who-isnt-bidding→OBS-004, mls/90887→null)
- `Research Concept` -> `src/app/institute/competition-gap/route.ts` (hand-built route; cover tag no longer "White Paper No. 1")
- `Research Backlog` -> `src/app/institute/route.ts` (gallery reframed; defers to `/research` up top)
- `Research agenda` -> `src/app/research/page.tsx` (agenda section links each concept back by awaited standard)
- `The editorial charter` -> `src/app/research/about/page.tsx` (the 3-rule charter)
- `The Institute` -> `src/lib/gov/shell.ts` (`/gov` nav "The Institute" now points to `/research`; adds a "Research Backlog" link)

Proof: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; probe rendered all 4 registry concepts (banner + Publication Status + correct OBS id/"not yet defined" + zero "White Paper No." on the cover). No deletes, no noindex, no migration.

### 2026-08-08 — In-modal signup (Homepage Readiness #56): first-time visitors never leave the map

The map's sign-in MODAL already handled email→password sign-in in-place, but "Create a free account" still did `toApp('&signup=1')` — a full page leave at the highest-friction onboarding moment (a first-time visitor clicking Save). Now signup stays in the modal (Step 3 email-first), the pending intent is QUEUED to localStorage BEFORE the email round-trip so it isn't lost, and the success state (Step 4) promises the OUTCOME ("your work is safe / waiting"), explains WHY (verify email, ~30s), and lets them keep browsing (more saves keep queueing). On return (signed in) a welcome-back toast fires. Reuses the existing `/api/auth/mindy-signup` — no new endpoint, all abuse/deliverability guards intact (email-first is the anti-abuse model, kept deliberately). OAuth/setup/forgot/MFA still redirect to /app (provider round-trip / token step — expected).

- `step(3)` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` (LOGIN_MODAL_HTML Steps 3+4, LOGIN_MODAL_JS doSignup/queueIntent/drainPendingIntents; `lgmCreate` no longer calls `toApp('&signup=1')`)
- `login-modal-signup` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/login-modal-signup.unit.test.ts` (8 asserts: 4 steps, no page-leave on create, real endpoint, queue-before-email, outcome-first copy, OAuth still redirects)

Proof: tsc clean; 51 files / 432 map tests green (incl. the new 8); LOGIN_MODAL_HTML div-balanced 16/16. Live render proof deferred to prod (worktree node_modules lacks the @swc/helpers subpath → local dev 500s; orthogonal to this change).

### 2026-08-08 — Map-native Settings drawer (Homepage Readiness #34): no more legacy escape hatch

The map account menu's "Settings" sent users to `/app?panel=settings` — a full leave into the legacy React app. Built a map-native drawer for the everyday, homepage-critical preferences (alert frequency · NAICS · keywords · target agencies), reusing the SAME APIs as /app: `GET /api/alerts/preferences` (read) + `POST /api/app/profile` with `precise:true` (write — canonical persist path, so a NAICS prefix can't fan into a whole family). Heavy account-admin (billing/security/SMS/team) stays as explicit /app links, deliberately not rebuilt. Two bugs caught by the live shape probe before shipping: (1) the GET wraps its payload in `data` — the drawer read the top level and would have rendered EMPTY; (2) the drawer offered only 5 of the 7 real `alert_frequency` values — a `mwf`/`tth` user would see no selection and a save would silently downgrade them.

- `openSettingsDrawer` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/settings-drawer.ts` (new string module: CSS/HTML/JS)
- `mindyAcctSettings` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/account-menu.ts` (Settings opens the drawer when present; /app fallback on favorites/saved)
- `SETTINGS_DRAWER_HTML` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` (injected before LOGIN_MODAL_HTML per the unclosed-div rule; JS before ACCOUNT_MENU_JS so openSettingsDrawer exists when the menu wires it)
- `map-native Settings drawer` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/settings-drawer.unit.test.ts` (8 asserts)

Proof: tsc clean; 52 files / 440 map tests green; drawer HTML div-balanced 17/17; live prod shape probe 8/8 (GET returns data.naicsCodes(11)/keywords(6)/targetAgencies(6)/frequency; POST accepts the write shape → "Profile updated successfully"). No new endpoint, no migration.

### 2026-08-08 — M-Estimate detail: real dated comps + value-history timeline (#88)

The M-Estimate detail showed a median + band + a $-DISTRIBUTION histogram, but NOT the individual comparable contracts behind the number, nor a TIME axis (the route even had a comment "we do NOT fake a per-YEAR timeline or multiple dated comps — fast-follow #88"). Built both from the SAME cached `recompete_opportunities` table + the SAME filter as `opp_value_range` (so the list, the year-series, the histogram and the median all describe ONE comparable set — they can't disagree). Two new RPCs: `opp_value_comps` (top-N dated awards: incumbent · $ · year · sub-agency, newest-first) + `opp_value_timeline` (MEDIAN award $ per year, min-2-per-year floor so a 1-contract year isn't a "trend"). Fetched at the winning tier alongside the histogram; both degrade to `undefined` on a missing/errored RPC → the drawer renders exactly as today (no comps/timeline sections, never a fabricated one).

- `opp_value_comps` / `opp_value_timeline` -> `supabase/migrations/20260808_m_estimate_comps_timeline.sql` (Eric-run — pooler password stale; code degrades clean until it lands)
- `ValueComp` / `ValueTimelinePoint` -> `src/lib/opportunities/value-range.ts` (fetchComps/fetchTimeline, same-tier Promise.all)
- `comps` / `timeline` pass-through -> `src/lib/opportunities/opp-intel.ts` (comparable_awards source only — a predecessor is ONE contract, not a set)
- `function vrTimeline` / `function vrComps` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` (render + CSS; wired into mEstMethodologyHTML)
- `opp_value_comps` -> `scripts/verify-m88.mjs` (live proof, run post-migration)

Proof: tsc clean; 24 lib tests (6 new #88 + 18 existing value-range/intel, no regression) + 440 map tests green; `verify-m88.mjs` correctly FAILS pre-migration (RPCs absent → the exact clean-degrade path). Post-migration live proof pending Eric running the migration.

### 2026-08-08 — M-Estimate detail on the PSC tier (#88 follow-up): comps/timeline now render on most opps

#88 shipped comps + timeline, but a live prod probe caught that they (and the pre-existing distribution chart) were HIDDEN on the PSC tier — and most real opps carry a PSC (7G21, DA10, …), so the band went PSC-scoped and the detail almost never showed. The three detail RPCs were NAICS/agency-keyed only, so they were deliberately skipped when the band was PSC-scoped (to avoid a NAICS-comps-vs-PSC-band mismatch). Eric: add PSC-scoped detail instead. Added `p_psc` (+ `p_psc_prefix_len`, default 2 = FSC group) to `opp_value_histogram` / `opp_value_comps` / `opp_value_timeline` using the EXACT PSC-family CASE `opp_value_range` already uses (`20260803_value_range_psc`), and value-range now passes the same `p_psc` to all three on the PSC tier → they MATCH the band and render.

- `p_psc` -> `supabase/migrations/20260808_m_estimate_detail_psc_tier.sql` (Eric-run; base RPCs already exist, this CREATE OR REPLACEs them with the added arg)
- `const pscArg = byPsc ? psc : null` -> `src/lib/opportunities/value-range.ts` (removed the `byPsc ? [undefined×3]` skip; three helpers take pscArg, forward p_psc only when set — additive, pre-migration errors → undefined → band intact)
- `opp_value_comps accepts p_psc` -> `scripts/verify-m88-psc.mjs` (live proof, run post-migration)
- `#88 PSC-tier detail` -> `src/lib/opportunities/value-comps-psc.unit.test.ts` (3 asserts)

Diagnosis note: the earlier "prod shows no comps" reads were NOT a bug — they were the PSC tier working as designed (median 654606 = `basis "541512 · PSC DA10"`, all detail correctly omitted). The pure-NAICS tier always returned comps(10)+timeline(13); this follow-up makes the PSC tier do so too.

Proof: tsc clean; 18 lib tests (3 new + 15 existing); `verify-m88-psc.mjs` correctly FAILS pre-migration (RPCs lack p_psc = the clean-degrade path). Post-migration live proof pending Eric running the migration.

| 2026-08-14 | EVERY company drawer on the Network map showed "Network hiccup — try opening it again." | Eric screenshots (Boeing, Cottonwood) + a headless repro (IMPRES, MSSQQ551LG41). NOT a network fault: `/api/app/company-detail` returned HTTP 200 `success:true` with all 21 company fields. A live window.onerror/Promise.catch trap on prod caught the real error — `ReferenceError: companyScaleTier is not defined` at `companyHead` -> `companyRender`. `companyScaleTier`/`companyScaleTierChip` are defined in VIEWPORT_JS; the drawer calls them from DRAWER_JS, a SEPARATE `<script>` IIFE that cannot see that closure. The throw happened INSIDE the fetch `.then()`, so the outer `.catch()` swallowed it and called `drawerLoadError(0)` — and status 0 is the ONE branch that prints "Network hiccup", so a pure RENDER bug reported itself as a network drop on a 200. The list CARD chip kept rendering "Top tier" correctly the whole time (same VIEWPORT_JS scope) — a working list proves nothing about the drawer. Fix = bridge both fns onto `window` in VIEWPORT_JS (same pattern as `window.__mapMode`/`window.__players`) + point the two DRAWER_JS call sites at `window.companyScaleTier`. NOT fixed by narrowing/moving the shared fn — the card chip at 1957 depends on it in its own scope. Swept DRAWER_JS for other cross-scope calls: none (the one `_uemail` hit is guarded by try/catch + a `typeof` check). | `window.companyScaleTier=companyScaleTier` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; full production build exit 0 (compiled, 564/564 static pages); vitest 535/535 across 63 map files (incl. existing network-drawer-dispatch 7/7, no regression); new `company-drawer-scale-tier-scope.unit.test.ts` PROVEN by inject->red->revert->green (injecting the original bare call fails 4/4 with the exact prod ReferenceError) | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-14 | Opportunity Map NAICS filter silently returned WRONG results for multi-code and word input | Eric: "the ui when you add multiple naics codes, people don't know to put a comma". The Filters NAICS box was FREETEXT whose value became the query verbatim (`FILT.naics=input.value`), and the reader split on ',' ONLY. Its placeholder showed a SINGLE code ("e.g. 236220 or a word like construction") and nothing said multiple codes need commas — so the natural "541512 541611" parsed as ONE junk token, and typing a word without picking from the autocomplete sent the literal word ("construction") as a NAICS filter. BOTH returned confidently wrong results with NO signal (the worst failure mode; a 200 with plausible pins). Not a query bug — `applyMapFilters` was always correct, which is why the `verify:oracles` filters check passed throughout. Fix = CHIP input: resolved codes render as chips, the text box is only for typing. Contract (Eric): natural-language lookup STAYS but chips are codes only; a word must be PICKED from the directory; codes separated by comma/space/newline auto-chip; every auto-chipped code is VERIFIED against /api/suggest-codes (999999 rejected, a <=4 prefix chips AS the prefix since the shared filter does a LIKE match); leftover unresolved text BLOCKS Apply (readDeep returns false, panel stays open with an inline error) so the UI cannot silently produce incorrect results. FILT.naics stays the comma-joined resolved string, so the fetch URLs and the Industry-dropdown two-controls-one-FILT sync are unchanged (the sync now writes through chips.set()). PSC keeps the old wireCodeAc for now. | `window.__naicsChips=wireChipCodes` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc exit 0; full production build exit 0 (compiled, 564/564 static pages); vitest 539/539 across 63 map files (8 new, 531 existing, no regression); new `naics-chip-input.unit.test.ts` PROVEN by inject->red->revert->green; BROWSER-verified against the real built page: "541512 541611 " -> 2 chips + value "541512,541611", bogus 999999 -> "Not a NAICS code: 999999" with existing chips intact, 0 page errors | PENDING deploy |

I would do it the day before or the night before"*) — because a plain TTL still makes SOMEONE eat the slow path at every expiry, and on demo day that someone could be the person demoing. TTL set to 6h, deliberately LONGER than the 3h refresh, so a missed cron degrades to slightly-older REAL numbers rather than back to a 2.8s page. Honesty guards: a DEGRADED compute is never cached (it would freeze a broken block into the front page for a whole TTL), an empty featured list is never cached, stale-on-error serves the last good payload, and the cron REPORTS `cached:false` when a warm-up ran but didn't stick. ⚠️ Two env traps hit while inserting the cron row, both worth knowing: a loose regex for **SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY** matches **PLANNER_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY** (a DIFFERENT project's key) first, yielding "Invalid API key"; and the values are quote-wrapped. Precompute route proven on prod BEFORE scheduling (200 + `application/json`, `cached:true`, `degraded:false`, ms≈3000 absorbed by the cron); unauth → 401. `cron_jobs` row `precompute-today-intel` `0 */3 * * *` inserted AFTER that proof and verified by an independent read-only query. | LIVE |

Guard PROVEN by inject→red→revert→green (restoring `esc(o.estMedian)` fails 2 tests). | 2026-08-15 | The BQ awards freshness oracle was BLIND, and reported blindness as a data failure | Eric prioritized this above another feature, with the right reasoning: *"your homepage now says Today's Intel. If the freshness oracle is blind, the homepage eventually becomes wrong. That's one of the few things that could damage trust."* DIAGNOSED (and my first hypothesis was WRONG — I assumed a runaway consumer): the project carries a MANUAL `QueryUsagePerDay` override of **2,097,152 MiB/day = 2 TiB**, against a 200 TiB default — a 100× reduction, set as a cost control. Once exhausted, EVERY BigQuery query fails instantly at **0 bytes billed**, so the entire visible job history is victims (measured: 118 of 120 recent jobs `quotaExceeded`, 0.00GB billed, `mindy-bq-reader` only) and the actual consumer that burned the 2 TiB is invisible behind them. Two dead ends worth recording: `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT` is denied to the read-only SA (`bigquery.jobs.listAll`, correct posture — routed around it with the user's own `bq` credentials), and `getJobs()` returns SHALLOW records whose statistics are empty until you fetch each job's metadata individually. Dry runs are FREE and work while the quota is blown — that is how the costs were measured (freshness 153MB, rollup-by-slug only 13MB, so the 70 rollup calls were NOT the cause). THE FIX is not the quota (raising it is a billing decision, Eric's to make in the GCP console) but the GUARD: "could not check" and "the data is stale" were reported identically, which is why this sat unresolved. The oracle now distinguishes them — a quota/rate-limit error falls back to the ingest's OWN stamp in Supabase `data_sources` (key `bq_awards`, stamped by a successful `npm run ingest:awards:apply`), which answers the question the oracle exists to answer ("did the weekly ingest run?") WITHOUT touching BigQuery. Result: the awards data is in fact HEALTHY — `last_built 2026-08-11`, 4 days into a weekly cadence — so the alarm was about the guard, not the data. | `STAMP_BUDGET_DAYS` -> `scripts/verify-oracles.mjs` | `npm run verify:oracles` **13/13 PASS** (first fully-green run this session; was 12/13 with freshness failing). The fallback is PROVEN to still fail loudly by inject→red→revert→green on BOTH failure modes: budget 21d→1d (simulating a stale ingest) FAILS, and pointing the stamp lookup at a non-existent row FAILS rather than silently passing. Only the genuinely-healthy case passes. Second independent guard confirmed live: the `check-data-freshness` cron ran `success` at 13:05 today and would have emailed the refresh checklist if `bq_awards` were overdue | PENDING deploy |

| 2026-08-15 | One runaway BigQuery scan could blind every data guard in the project for a day | Follow-on to the freshness diagnosis. Eric: *"I want to keep it small not incur super high runaway bill"* — so the fix is deliberately NOT raising the quota. PRICED IT FIRST (dry runs are free and work even while the daily quota is exhausted, which is the only reason any of this was measurable): the manual "QueryUsagePerDay" override is 2 TiB/day vs a 200 TiB default, and the measured heaviest realistic day is **~303 GB** — weekly ingest 275 GB + monthly rollup crons 18 GB + freshness ×24 3.7 GB + cached app/SEO ~6.5 GB. So the cap already carries **6.8× headroom and is NOT too small**; the GCP default would be a $37,500/mo ceiling vs the current $375. Two of my own hypotheses died against the numbers: the 70 "rollup:by-slug" calls are only **13 MB** each (not the cause), and the full weekly ingest is only **275 GB / 13% of one day** (also not the cause) — it costs about **$1.68/run, ~$7/month**. What CAN exhaust it is the "SELECT *" shape on the 63M-row awards table at **41 GiB per query** (~48 of them = the whole day), because BigQuery is columnar and "LIMIT 10" applies AFTER the scan. THE REAL DAMAGE IS NOT COST: when the daily quota blows, every query in the project fails instantly at **0 bytes billed**, so the freshness oracle goes blind AND the evidence is destroyed — all subsequent jobs log 0 bytes, hiding the actual consumer behind a wall of victims (118 of 120 recent jobs "quotaExceeded"). ⚠️ MY FIRST FIX WAS WRONG AND THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT IT: I proposed dropping the per-query ceiling 5 GB → 2 GB, but the heaviest LEGITIMATE runtime query (related-contractors aggregate) measures **4.48 GiB** — the old code comment understated it as "~3GB" — so 2 GiB would have broken a live feature to fix a problem it does not cause. Ceiling STAYS at 5 GiB; bulk jobs (ingest/rollup rebuilds, which legitimately scan everything) now opt out EXPLICITLY via a named "bulkJob" string, so a full-table scan is always a deliberate batch act and never something a request path can do by accident. STILL UNKNOWN, and stated as such: what actually burned the 2 TiB — the failure mode destroys its own evidence. Raising the override remains Eric's call in the GCP console; nothing here changes billing. | `RUNTIME_MAX_BYTES` -> `src/lib/bigquery/client.ts` | tsc 0; production build 0; vitest 25/25 on src/lib/bigquery (5 new). Guard PROVEN by inject→red→revert→green on BOTH regressions: dropping the ceiling to 2 GiB FAILS (catching my own first instinct), and removing the cap entirely FAILS 2. ⚠️ HONEST LIMIT recorded in the test file: that BigQuery REJECTS an over-ceiling query at execution is NOT verified here — `maximumBytesBilled` is not enforced during a dry run, and a real query currently fails on the exhausted daily quota first, which would mask the result and produce a FALSE PASS. The assertions lock the configuration; enforcement is BigQuery's documented behaviour | PENDING deploy |

| 2026-08-15 | The front page collapsed to "16 opportunities" every night, and the map overlays covered the map's own controls | Eric, mid-pass: "i am worried about ux not facts right now" — so these are all UX defects, though the first one looks like a data bug and is not. FOUR fixes. (1) THE MIDNIGHT COLLAPSE: the hero read "16 opportunities posted in the last 24 hours" at 00:13 UTC while the four preceding days held 1322 / 1961 / 2059 / 2156. posted_date is a DATE, midnight-stamped (measured: every row reads T00:00:00+00:00), so ">= now - 24h" really means ">= yesterday's calendar date" — minutes after UTC midnight only the notices already carrying yesterday's date qualify. Technically true, and it reads as a dead market on a page whose whole claim is that every figure is real; a visitor at 8pm ET sees the trough. Fixed by anchoring to the latest day with REAL VOLUME via latestPostedDay(). ⚠️ MY FIRST VERSION REPRODUCED THE BUG: anchoring to the merely-NEWEST date still returned 16, because the newest date is usually a PARTIAL day still filling — the MIN_MEANINGFUL_DAY floor (100; below a real weekend day at 270, above the trickle) is what actually fixes it. Result 16 → 1,338. All four user-facing labels moved from "in the last 24 hours" to "in the latest day of filings" so the words match the anchor. (2) + (3) THE MAP OVERLAYS COVERED THE MAP: Leaflet owns bottom-left (legend) and bottom-right (zoom + attribution); both overlays sat at bottom:24-28px, so the count pill covered the "Open · Recompete · Forecast" legend text and the CTA covered the +/- buttons — the map could not be zoomed from the front page. Undetectable from the DOM because the collision is with content inside a cross-origin iframe; caught by screenshot. Count moved to top-left beside LIVE, CTA lifted to bottom:74px. (4) THE CAPTION ARGUED WITH THE MAP: the pill read "16 posted today" over an UNFILTERED map — two different windows under one caption, the small number making a live market look dead. ⚠️ I briefly "fixed" this by adding ?posted=7 to the iframe, then found the map route reads ONLY `embed` server-side (route.ts:8139) — every other filter is client-side, so the param filtered NOTHING. A URL that reads like it works while doing nothing is worse than the mismatch it pretends to solve, so it was REMOVED and the pill now cites what the map genuinely shows: "34,827 open opportunities".
