| 2026-08-15 | The front-page map rendered 600 opportunities as ~35 visible dots | Eric: "make the map pins denser so it doesn't look empty". MEASURED FIRST, and the density was never the problem: the embed API returns 600 opportunities, ALL with real coordinates, and all 600 DO render (600 path.leaflet-interactive nodes in the DOM). They collapse onto just 76 DISTINCT coordinates, and 403 of them (67%) stack on ONE pixel over Columbus, Ohio — DLA parts buys ("PAWL, RIGHT HAND", "ENGINE BLOCK, DIESEL") pinned to the buying depot because SAM publishes no place-of-performance for them (397 of 400 sampled have pop_state NULL). The depot coordinate is the only honest one available, so the pins could not be truthfully spread; the fix had to make the stack LEGIBLE instead. ROOT CAUSE: PIN_JS was concatenated ONLY on the non-embed branch, and template.html calls its helpers behind typeof guards — "(typeof clusterRows==='function') ? clusterRows(...) : {singles:rows}" — so in the embed every guard SILENTLY took the fallback: no clustering, no mkPin, raw circleMarkers. Nothing errored, nothing logged, every guard "passed". ⚠️ A typeof guard that degrades silently HIDES a missing dependency rather than surfacing it — that is the transferable lesson here. Fix: inject PIN_JS + VTAG_CSS into the embed branch, and gate the thresholds on a new __EMBED_CLUSTER__ flag so the INTERACTIVE map keeps the Zillow overlapping-dots model chosen 2026-08-12 (this is a front-page legibility change, not a reversal of that decision). TWO SELF-INFLICTED REGRESSIONS CAUGHT BY MEASURING, both mid-fix: (a) shipping PIN_JS activated pinTooFar, whose PIN_DOT_ZOOM=5 floor blanked the embed completely at its CONUS 4.5 boot — 0 pins and a "Zoom in to see opportunities" prompt on a static hero the visitor cannot zoom, so PIN_DOT_ZOOM drops to 0 in the embed only; (b) setting REGIONAL_ZOOM=12 turned every lone opportunity into a bubble reading "1" — a bubble labelled 1 is just a dot that has learned to count — so it stays 0 and only real stacks get bubbles. Result: 10 cluster bubbles (414 / 126 / 21 / 9 / 3 / 2s) + 17 single dots, zero "1" bubbles. | `__EMBED_CLUSTER__` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc 0; production build 0; client-JS syntax 10/10; vitest 620/620 across opportunity-map + today + lib/today (576 map tests green = no regression to the interactive map). Guards PROVEN by inject→red→revert→green on BOTH defects. ⚠️ THE FIRST VERSION OF THE PIN_JS GUARD WAS A FALSE PASS — asserting the bare token "PIN_JS" stayed green with the injection deleted, because the word also appears in the adjacent comment; tightened to assert the actual concatenation after stripping comments, then re-proven red. Live-verified INSIDE the iframe (the parent DOM cannot see cross-origin frame content — an earlier parent-scoped query reported a false zero and briefly looked like a broken fix) | PENDING deploy |

| 2026-08-17 | Three map controls were DEAD JAVASCRIPT on prod — Industry filter, "All industries", and the Today's Lens clear | Eric's list read like UI organization work ("fix Agency/Office/State to auto-populate"); investigating found three controls that were not mis-organized but BROKEN, and a browser confirmed it on live getmindy.ai before a line was changed. (1) THE INDUSTRY MENU WAS INERT. Clicking "All industries" threw `PAGEERROR: commitLive is not defined` on prod. `commitLive()` is declared inside the AGENCY dropdown's IIFE (route.ts:2998) and called from the INDUSTRY one (route.ts:3098) — function declarations are function-scoped, not `<script>`-tag-scoped, so the call never resolved. There is no try/catch on that handler, so it aborted after `reflectWorking()`: the rows cleared visually while FILT.naics/FILT.psc kept their old values and the map never refetched. The documented "the All row IS the clear" behaviour did not work at all. (2) WORSE, AND ONLY VISIBLE ONCE (1) WAS READ CAREFULLY: the individual industry rows never committed EITHER. Row onclick called only `syncHdr()` — no commit, no refetch — and the Apply button had been deliberately removed from the markup (the "live filtering, no Apply button" redesign at route.ts:331-333). So checking Construction updated nothing, ever. The Industry filter was functionally dead in both directions, not merely on its clear row. ⚠️ MY FIRST FIX REPRODUCED THE EXACT BUG I WAS FIXING: wiring the rows to `commitLive(); reflectAllRow();` — `reflectAllRow` is ALSO an Agency-IIFE local (route.ts:2994). Caught by grepping every occurrence before building, not after. Each IIFE now owns its own `commitLive`, its own debounce timer and its own `reflectAllRow`; nothing reaches across. (3) THE TODAY'S LENS ✕ SILENTLY DID NOTHING (route.ts:7531): DRAWER_JS called `readDeep(); fetchView();` bare, and both are VIEWPORT_JS locals invisible from that block, so a ReferenceError fired and the surrounding catch swallowed it — the boxes unchecked, the pill stayed, the map stayed filtered. This is the SAME bug class the file already warns about 20 lines above, where the APPLY path was correctly fixed to guard on `window.__applyStrategyBoxes` and the CLEAR path was missed. Now routed through the same bridge. (4) THE LOGGED-OUT HEADER: `account-menu.ts:90` decoded `split(".")[0]` — the JWT HEADER, which carries no email — so `tokEmail()` always fell through to `briefings_access_email` and returned "" whenever that key was absent, and the gate was `if(!t||!em)`, requiring BOTH. A genuinely signed-in user with no legacy key rendered as logged out. A CORRECT decoder already exists in the same codebase (`decodeEmail`, route.ts:7240, reading `[1]`) and every other gate migrated to token-only (route.ts:5634, 2639) — this was the last surface on the bad decode. Fixed to `[1]` + gate on the TOKEN only. Eric's actual ask was the visible half: the top-right showed a bare 20px person glyph whose only label was a hover `title`, so it now renders a labelled "Log In" pill, prefers the in-page `openSignInModal` that every other gated action already uses, and carries `location.search` in `?next=` so filters/lens/viewport survive the round trip. NOT DONE HERE, deliberately: the Agency/Buying-Office/State auto-populate work and the icon standardisation (8 stroke-widths, 17 sizes, 6 mechanisms, 14 Unicode glyphs used as icons) are separate changes — Buying Office and State have NO option list at all today, which is a build, not a fix. | `function commitLive` -> `src/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc 0; production build 0; client-JS syntax 10/10 templates parse clean; vitest 625/625 across src/app/opportunity-map (76 files). ⚠️ THE UNIT TESTS WERE NEVER THE PROOF AND COULD NOT HAVE BEEN — all 625 were green while all three controls were dead on prod, because they are source-string assertions and this is a runtime scope failure. Proof is a real headless browser: BEFORE on live getmindy.ai captured `PAGEERROR: commitLive is not defined` on the All-industries click; AFTER against a local production build the same click emits ZERO page errors and fires a refetch (1 call to /api/app/opportunity-map), an individual row click (Construction) likewise emits zero errors and fires a refetch, and the logged-out button computes to text "Log In", class mindy-acct-signin, aria-label "Log in", width 69.8px, border-radius 8px. The only console noise in the local run is HTTP 500 from the data API, which is the local env lacking prod credentials — the same request returns 200 on getmindy.ai | PENDING deploy |
| 2026-08-17 | The map's pin endpoint cost ~3s on EVERY pan/zoom — and the cost was fixed, not proportional to what you were looking at | Eric: "investigate and resolve map lag/latency issues." MEASURED FIRST, and the measurement is what located the bug: `/api/app/opportunity-map` took 3.47s for the whole US (968 pins), 3.03s for DC metro (673 pins) and **3.65s for a single city block returning THREE pins** — a request 300x smaller cost the SAME. That flatness is the whole diagnosis: the cost cannot be payload, geometry, or pin count. Control on the same tiny bbox: `grants-map` 0.51s, platform baseline 0.37s — so ~2.9s was fixed cost inside this one route. ROOT CAUSE: `countUniqueListingsForFilters()`. The headline count is deliberately VIEWPORT-INDEPENDENT (no bbox, so it reconciles with the dashboard), and PostgREST cannot `count(distinct)`, so it fetches the canonical-key columns for the entire filtered corpus and dedupes in JS. That part is correct. What was not correct is that it walked ~155,629 mappable rows **1,000 at a time in a SEQUENTIAL for-loop** — ~156 round-trips, each awaiting the previous — to produce ONE number, on every single request, with `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` so nothing cached. `fetchView()` fans out to up to 4 horizon endpoints per pan, so this route gated the entire interaction. FIX: the first page already carries `count: 'exact'`, so ONE round-trip reveals how many pages exist and the remainder are fetched CONCURRENTLY. Same rows, same order, same key, same dedupe. ⚠️ WHAT I DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO: rewrite this as a SQL RPC doing a DB-side distinct count over the coalesced canonical key. I verified in the live DB that SQL reproduces the number exactly (155,629 raw → 110,351 distinct), and it would collapse 156 round-trips to 1 — strictly faster than what shipped. It is still the wrong change here: `applyMapFilters` is the ONE filter builder shared by this viewport query AND saved-search alerts, and re-expressing its predicates in SQL forks the definition of "what matches" — exactly the drift the filters oracle exists to catch. The pagination was the bug; the filter path is not mine to duplicate. A DB-side count belongs in the shared lib, as its own change. ⚠️ A null `count` is UNKNOWN, not zero (Bug Prevention Rule #11) — it falls back to the original sequential walk rather than silently returning whatever the first page held. | `PAGED IN PARALLEL` -> `src/app/api/app/opportunity-map/route.ts` | tsc 0; production build 0; vitest 190/190 across src/lib/opportunities; `verify:oracles --only filters` GREEN (all 8 filters still narrow to genuinely-matching rows, baseline 12,679 — the guard that the shared filter path was not forked). CORRECTNESS PROVEN BY DIFF against live prod, not by inspection: `totalForFilters` is **9877 on both** for a tiny bbox and for the whole US, with pins/totalInView identical (3/3 and 968/968). SPEED, measured 3 runs each: whole-US 2.92-2.98s -> 0.49-0.57s, tiny-bbox 2.97-3.28s -> 0.64-0.81s. ⚠️ HONEST ADJUSTMENT — that headline OVERSTATES the fix: local runs skip Vercel's network/cold-start. A control on `grants-map`, which this change does not touch, measures local 0.40-0.47s vs prod 1.12-1.18s, so ~0.7s of every delta is environment. The real per-request saving is ~1.7-2.4s, not 2.4s. Final prod numbers must be re-measured after deploy | PENDING deploy |

Both numbers were WRONG. PostgREST hard-caps a response at 1000 rows NO MATTER WHAT the limit says, so it counted only the first 1000: the real figures are 209 offices and 912 open. A wrong count beside a real office name is worse than no picker — it reads as authoritative. | 2026-08-17 | Local branches had grown to 516 while the remote was already clean — and the setting everyone reaches for was ALREADY ON | Eric: "enable auto delete on merge since we keep accumulating branches." CHECKED BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING: `delete_branch_on_merge` was already TRUE, and the evidence says it works — ZERO merged branches remained on origin. The accumulation was 100% LOCAL, because GitHub deleting its copy of a branch never deletes yours and no repo setting can. So the fix is local tooling, not a settings flip. Pruned 79 local branches (516 -> 437), every one with 0 commits outside origin/main, none pinned by a worktree. ⚠️ THE GUARD THAT MATTERS: "the remote is gone" is NOT evidence the work landed. A SQUASH merge rewrites the commit, so the original ref never looks merged — measured here, 39 of 44 branches whose remote was already auto-deleted STILL held unmerged commits. Only the commit count decides, and the script uses that alone. ⚠️ CAUGHT MID-TASK: an awk over the human `git worktree list` output grabbed the ahead/behind MARKER column instead of branch names, producing a delete list of five "+" characters. It deleted nothing (no branch is named +), but a subtler mis-parse would not have announced itself — so the script parses `--porcelain`, never the human format. Also kept: 2 branches `git branch -d` REFUSED because their upstream holds commits the local ref lacks; that refusal is information, so the tool uses -d and never -D, and reports what it kept. Shipped as `npm run tidy:branches` (DRY-RUN by default, `-- --go` deletes) + a `post-merge` hook that only REPORTS on main — a hook that silently deletes refs is how hooks get disabled. | `tidy-branches` -> `scripts/tidy-branches.mjs` | Script proven by running it: dry-run lists exactly the 2 prunable branches; `--go` deletes 0 and correctly reports both as kept (git -d refused); the worktree guard excludes all 27 pinned branches INCLUDING the in-flight ones. Hook proven by running it IN PLACE with the branch forced to main — prints the report; on a topic branch it is silent. ⚠️ TWO EARLIER "it is silent" RESULTS WERE TEST ARTIFACTS, not bugs: a copy run from /tmp made `dirname` resolve ROOT outside the repo, and a run in the main checkout hit the `[ -f scripts/tidy-branches.mjs ]` guard because the script is not merged there yet. Verified the real path before believing either | PENDING deploy |
